Full 72-slot cylinder (CSM_V3_Cylinder_V1_1_FULL.stl)\n  2. 30 deg test wedge (CSM_V3_Cylinder_V1_1_TESTWEDGE.stl)\n     - Tests 3 slot widths: 1.20 / 1.22 / 1.25mm\n     - Includes spring groove for full validation\n  3. Top + side blueprint (Cylinder_V1_1_Blueprint.svg)\n\n==========================================\nTEST WEDGE STRATEGY (PRINT FIRST!)\n==========================================\n\nThe test wedge validates BOTH:\n  1. Slot fit (3 slot widths to test)\n  2. Spring groove fit (when springs arrive)\n\nPHASE 1 - Print test wedge (don't wait for springs)\n  Print: CSM_V3_Cylinder_V1_1_TESTWEDGE.stl\n  Time: 30-45 minutes\n  Material: ~12g PETG\n  \n  Test now:\n    - Insert needle in each slot pair (1.20/1.22/1.25 mm)\n    - Verify slide motion is smooth\n    - Pick winning slot width\n    - Measure groove with caliper (should be ~2.9mm wide after PETG shrink)\n\nPHASE 2 - When springs arrive\n  Test:\n    - Try fitting a length of spring wire into the groove\n    - Should sit in groove with ~half wire diameter exposed\n    - If too tight: file groove slightly OR adjust SPRING_GROOVE_W\n    - If too loose: re-print with reduced groove width\n\nPHASE 3 - Full cylinder\n  Once both slot AND groove are validated:\n    - Edit SLOT_WIDTH if needed (1.22 default)\n    - Edit SPRING_GROOVE_W if needed (3.0 default)\n    - Regenerate full cylinder STL\n    - Print full cylinder (4-6 hours, ~120g PETG)\n\n==========================================\nPRINT SETTINGS (PETG)\n==========================================\n  Material:      PETG (DRY filament)\n  Layer height:  0.2mm\n  Perimeters:    5\n  Top/bottom:    6 solid layers\n  Infill:        40% Gyroid or Cubic\n  Orientation:   Cylinder UPRIGHT (axis Z)\n  Supports:      NONE (slots and groove are vertical/horizontal)\n  Speed:         40mm/s walls, 120mm/s infill\n  Cooling:       Fan ON\n  Bed adhesion:  Brim or skirt\n\n==========================================\nASSEMBLY ORDER (with spring)\n==========================================\n1. Apply Loctite 603 to M5 bolt threads\n2. Place cylinder onto Drive Hub V1.3 (boss enters pocket)\n3. Insert 4 M5 bolts through cylinder counterbores into hub\n4. Tighten cross-pattern to ~5 Nm\n5. Wait 24h for Loctite cure\n6. Insert all 72 needles from TOP of cylinder\n7. Wrap cylinder spring around the OD groove:\n   - Open/straight spring goes around cylinder\n   - Twist ends together to close loop\n   - Spring should sit in groove, ~half exposed\n   - Should provide light radial pressure on needle butts\n8. Verify all 72 needles slide freely with spring engaged\n9. Spin cylinder by hand - should rotate smoothly\n\n==========================================\nHARDWARE STATUS\n==========================================\n\nALREADY HAVE:\n  - PETG filament\n  - 4x M5 bolts (via screw assortment kit B0FFSNFK3Y)\n  - Loctite 603 (B0074NALBO)\n  - 100x Legare 12g needles (FlyDesigns)\n\nTO ORDER:\n  - 3x Cylinder Springs Common Thickness (FlyDesigns, $18 + ship)\n    * Order with note: \"Please ship OPEN/STRAIGHT for sizing\"\n\n==========================================\nNEXT STEPS AFTER CYLINDER\n==========================================\n\nPhase 1 (this delivery): Cylinder V1.1\nPhase 2 (next): Sinker Ring V1.0 (72 sinkers + 6 yarn windows + taper)\nPhase 3 (next): Cam Ring V6 (V5 with bore change to ID 115mm)\nPhase 4 (next): Retainer Ring V1.0 (PA12, 6 yarn cutouts)\nPhase 5 (next): Cassette Base V1.0 (200mm OD, 6 feeder mount pads)\nPhase 6 (later): Feeder Module V1.0 (mechanical + servo-ready)\nPhase 7 (later): Yarn Mast V1.0 (2-cone start, expandable to 6)\n\nESTIMATED PROJECT TIMELINE:\n  Phase 1: Done (cylinder design)\n  Phase 2-5: ~2-3 weeks design + print\n  Phase 6-7: ~2-4 weeks (after spring arrives)\n  Total to first knit: 6-8 weeks\nEOF\n\ncd csm_cylinder_v11\nzip -r /mnt/user-data/outputs/CSM_V3_Cylinder_V1_1_DELIVERY.zip 3D-Project -q\n\necho \"=== ZIP CONTENTS ===\"\nunzip -l /mnt/user-data/outputs/CSM_V3_Cylinder_V1_1_DELIVERY.zip\necho \"\"\nls -lh /mnt/user-data/outputs/CSM_V3_Cylinder_V1_1_DELIVERY.zip",

VERY tight\\n\\n  Designed 3.2mm -> printed ~3.05-3.10mm\\n  Wire 2.79mm fits in 3.05mm with 0.26mm clearance - GOOD\\n\\nReviewer is right - my original was too tight after PETG shrink.\\n\\nVERDICT: ACCEPT width 3.2mm\\n\\n===========================================================================\\nFINAL SPRING GROOVE PARAMETERS (REVISED)\\n===========================================================================\\n\\nFinal V1.1.1 spring groove:\\n  Width (axial):  3.2mm  (was 3.0)  - The reviewer didn't show derivation.\\n\\nSpring stiffness k (claimed): 0.8-1.5 N/mm 'empirical'\\nCam lift: 1.0mm\\n\\nF = k * deflection\\nFor 1mm deflection, F = 0.8-1.5 N (matches Case B)\\nFor 1.5mm deflection (deeper recess?), F = 1.2-2.25 N\\n\\nWait, the reviewer says deeper recess = STIFFER, but the math is unclear.\\nDeeper recess doesn't necessarily mean stiffer - it just means less\\nof the wire is exposed. The k value depends on wire diameter, coil geometry,\\nnot how recessed it is.\\n\\nHOWEVER - deeper recess COULD mean:\\n  - Wire constrained by groove walls more (less free flex)\\n  - Need more force to push wire OUT of groove\\n  - Effective stiffness higher\\n\\nThis is plausible but the reviewer's specific numbers (1.2-1.8 N)\\naren't derived rigorously.\\n\\nConclusion: The DIRECTION is correct (shallower = more compliance).\\nThe exact MAGNITUDE is uncertain.\\n\\nEither way, accepting reviewer's recommendation is safer.\\nIf too loose, easy to shim or print V1.2 with deeper groove.\\nIf too stiff, harder to remove material from already-printed part.\\n\\nERR ON THE SIDE OF: less recessed = more flex = safer\\n\",\"stderr\":\"\"}"

But the actual cylinder spring is also constrained by:\n- Hoop tension around the full 360\u00b0 loop\n- Distributed contact across 72 needles\n- Groove walls at both top and bottom\n\nSo the **real-world stiffness reduction is probably 20-35%, not the full 47%** \u2014 but the direction is absolutely correct, and the conclusion holds: V1.1.1 has meaningfully more compliance than V1.1.\n\n## Why V1.1.1 Is the Right Choice (Reinforced)\n\nThe reviewer's analysis confirms **we made the right risk-reward call**:\n\n| If I was wrong about V1.1.1 (too soft) | If I was wrong about V1.1 (too stiff) |\n|---|---|\n| Print test wedge \u2192 needles don't snap back | Print full cylinder \u2192 needles bind |\n| Time cost: ~1 hour | Time cost: 4-6 hours |\n| Material cost: ~10g PETG | Material cost: ~120g PETG |\n| Reprint wedge with depth 1.35 (between 1.2 and 1.5) | Reprint full cylinder |\n\n**V1.1.1 fails cheap. V1.1 would fail expensive.** That's what good engineering risk management looks like.\n\n## Red Flags to Watch During Test Wedge Testing\n\n| Behavior | Diagnosis | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| \u2705 Smooth motion + clear snap-back | Spring tension correct | Print full cylinder |\n| \u26a0\ufe0f Easy motion, no snap-back | Spring too soft (V1.1.1 risk) | Reprint with depth 1.35mm |\n| \u26a0\ufe0f Binding or high force | Spring too stiff (unlikely with V1.1.1) | Reprint with depth 1.0mm |\n| \u26a0\ufe0f Inconsistent between needles | Print quality variation | Check filament/temperature |\n\n## No Macro Changes Needed\n\nThis review **validates V1.1.1**, doesn't ask for changes. The reviewer's conclusion: *\"This is a 'print-first, tune-second' design \u2014 which is the right strategy.\"*\n\nThat's exactly what our test wedge approach is designed for. **Cylinder V1.1.1 is locked.**\n\n## Where We Are\n\n| Part | Version | Status |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Cylinder** | **V1.1.1** | \u2705 **DONE \u2014 locked** |\n| Sinker Ring | V1.0 | Not started |\n| Cam Ring | V6 (from V5) | Not started |\n| Retainer Ring | V1.0 | Not started |\n| Cassette Base | V1.0 | Not started |\n| Feeder Module | V1.0 | Not started |\n| Yarn Mast | V1.0 | Not started |\n\n## Status of the Drive Subsystem (parallel \u2014 already done)\n\n| Part | Version | Status |\n|---|---|---|\n| Bearing Housings | V2.5.1 | \u2705 Print-ready |\n| Drive Hub | V1.3 | \u2705 Print-ready |\n| Motor Mount | V1.3 | \u2705 Print-ready |\n| Spacer Tubes (3\u00d7) | V2.5.1 | \u2705 Print-ready |\n\n## Ready for Next Part\n\n**My recommendation: Sinker Ring V1.0 next.**\n\nReasons:\n- Most geometrically complex remaining part (72 sinkers + 6 taper zones)\n- Highest validation risk (small features at PETG resolution)\n- Critical to knitting mechanics (without sinkers, no stitch formation)\n- Independent of springs (can be designed without waiting)\n\n**Other options if you'd prefer:**\n- **Cam Ring V6** \u2014 simpler (V5 with bore change to ID 115mm)\n- **Retainer Ring V1.0** \u2014 simpler (PA12 ring with 6 cutouts)\n- **Cassette Base V1.0** \u2014 structural (200mm OD, 6 mount pads)\n\nTell me which to tackle next, and I'll start coding.",
